) of length
\Delta=\Delta(N)
, i.e. for
N\geq N_{0}
independent of
z
large enough,

\Delta<\frac{1}{N^{1/3}k\bar{\mu}\bar{h}}.

For
j
,
r=0,\dots,M-1
and
\tau_{j},t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]
, since for
|t-s|<\frac{1}{N^{1/3}k\bar{\mu}\bar{h}}
,
|\phi^{z}(t)-\phi^{z}(s)|<\frac{1}{N^{1/3}}
,
we have on
\tilde{F}^{N,z}

|Z^{N,z}(\tau_{j}-)-\phi^{z}(t)|\leq|Z^{N,z}(\tau_{j}-)-\phi^{z}(\tau_{j})|+|\phi^{z}(\tau_{j})-\phi^{z}(t)|\leq\frac{2}{N^{1/3}},

and hence

\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))-\tilde{\delta}_{N}\leq\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,z}(\tau_{j}-))\leq\sup_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))+\tilde{\delta}_{N}.

We compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}}\sum_{\tau}\log\beta_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,z}(\tau-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N,z}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,z}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,z}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\Big(\sup_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))+\tilde{\delta}_{N}\Big)\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N,z}}Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\log\bar{\beta}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}}Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\sup_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))\Big\{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N,z}}\big]\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}}\big]+\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N,z}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}})\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\log\bar{\beta}\Big\{\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}}\big]\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}}\big]+\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}})\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\tilde{\delta}_{N}k\bar{\mu}T

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N,z}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\mu_{j}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\Delta\sup_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\Big\{\log\bar{\beta}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\Big(|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N,z}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}})|+|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}})|\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\tilde{\delta}_{N}k\bar{\mu}T+\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}]k\bar{\mu}T\log\bar{\beta}\Big\}

(35)

\displaystyle\qquad=:\overline{S}^{N,z}+\overline{U}^{N,z},

where
\overline{S}^{N,z}
and
\overline{U}^{N,z}
are the first respectively the second term in Inequality (
35
). In a similar fashion we obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}}\sum_{\tau}\log\beta_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,z}(\tau-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N,z}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\mu_{j}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\Delta\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}\Big\{\log\underline{\beta}\sum_{j,\,\mu_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\Big(|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N,z}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}})|+|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r},t_{r+1}})|\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\tilde{\delta}_{N}k\bar{\mu}T+\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}]k\bar{\mu}T\log\underline{\beta}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad=:\underline{S}^{N,z}+\underline{U}^{N,z};

we first note that
\overline{U}^{N,z},\underline{U}^{N,z}\rightarrow 0
as
N\rightarrow\infty
uniformly in
z
, since
\tilde{\delta}_{N}\rightarrow 0
and

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N,z}]\rightarrow 0
as
N\rightarrow 0
uniformly in
z
. Furthermore, as (up to a factor which converges to
1
uniformly in
z
)
\overline{S}^{N,z}
and
\underline{S}^{N,z}
are upper respectively lower Riemann sums, we obtain

\overline{S}^{N,z},\underline{S}^{N,z}\rightarrow\sum_{j}\mu_{j}\int_{0}^{T}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{z}(t))dt

(36)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
; since

|\overline{S}^{N,z}-\underline{S}^{N,z}|\leq 2\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N,z}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}]}k\bar{\mu}\tilde{\delta}_{N}\rightarrow 0

uniformly in
z
, the convergence in (
36
) is likewise uniform in
z
.

The uniform convergence implies (cf. (
31
) - (
34
) and the preceding discussion) that

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\Big(\inf_{z\in A,\,|x-z|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\phi^{x}(t)|<\epsilon\big]\Big)\geq-\sup_{z\in A,\,|x-z|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\int_{0}^{T}\ell(\phi^{z}(t),\mu)dt

In combination with the uniform continuity of
\ell(\cdot,\mu)
(recall the boundedness of the
\log\beta_{j}
) this proves the assertion.
∎

The main building block for the lower bound is the following result.

Theorem 5.2
.

Assume that
\log\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) is bounded and continuous. Let
\phi\in D([0,T];A)
with
\phi(0)=x
and
\epsilon>0
. Then,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\geq-I_{T,x}(\phi).

The convergence is uniform in
x\in A
.

Proof.

We can w.l.o.g. assume that
I_{T,x}(\phi)<\infty
(and hence
\phi
is absolutely continuous) as else the assertion is trivial. We approximate the function
\phi
by a continuous piecewise linear function and then apply the LDP for linear functions to each of these linear functions. To this end, we let
\delta>0
and divide the interval
[0,T]
into
J
subintervals of length
\Delta=T/J
,
[t_{r-1},t_{r}]
(
r=1,\dots,J
) such that the resulting piecewise linear approximation

\tilde{\phi}(t)=\phi(t_{r-1})+\frac{t-t_{r-1}}{\Delta}(\phi(t_{r})-\phi(t_{r-1}))

satisfies

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\phi(t)-\tilde{\phi}(t)|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}

(recall that
\phi
is continuous).

We now apply Theorem
4.18
twice (in Inequalities (
37
) and (
39
)) and choose
J
large enough in order to assure

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt

\displaystyle=\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}}^{t_{r}}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}}^{t_{r}}L(\phi(t_{r-1}),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt-\frac{\delta}{4}

(37)

\displaystyle\geq\Delta\sum_{r=1}^{J}L\Big(\phi(t_{r-1}),\frac{\Delta\phi(t_{r})}{\Delta}\Big)-\frac{\delta}{4}

(38)

\displaystyle\geq\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}}^{t_{r}}L\big(\tilde{\phi}(t),\tilde{\phi}^{\prime}(t)\big)dt-\frac{\delta}{2},

(39)

where

\Delta\phi(t_{r}):=\phi(t_{r})-\phi(t_{r-1}).

Note that for Inequality (
38
), we have applied Jensen’s inequality and the fact that
L
is convex in its second argument (cf. Corollary
4.10
).
As
I_{T,x}(\tilde{\phi})<\infty
, this implies

\Delta\phi(t_{r})\in\mathcal{C}\quad\text{for all }r.

We note that by the continuity of
L(\cdot,y)
,
\mu^{*}(x,y)
(the minimizing
\mu\in V_{x,y}=\tilde{V}_{y}
for
\ell(x,\cdot)
) is “almost optimal” for all
\tilde{x}
sufficiently close to
x
(in the sense that
\ell(\tilde{x},\mu^{*}(x,y))
is close to
L(\tilde{x},y)
). By dividing each interval
[t_{r-1},t_{r}]
into further subintervals
[s_{j-1},s_{j}]
if necessary, we can hence represent the directions
\Delta\phi(t_{k})/\Delta
by

\mu^{j}\in V_{\tilde{\phi}(t),\Delta\phi(t_{r})/\Delta}=\tilde{V}_{\Delta\phi(t_{r})/\Delta}

in such a way that

L\Big(\tilde{\phi}(t),\frac{\Delta\phi(t_{r})}{\Delta}\Big)\geq\ell(\tilde{\phi}(t),\mu^{j})-\frac{\delta}{4T}\quad\text{for all }t\in[s_{j-1},s_{j}].

For simplicity of exposition, we assume that this further subdivision of the intervals
[t_{r-1},t_{r}]
is not required and denote the “almost optimal”
\mu
’s by
\mu^{r}
(
r=1,\dots,J
).
Hence

\int_{0}^{T}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt\geq\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}}^{t_{r}}\ell(\tilde{\phi}(t),\mu^{r})-\frac{3\delta}{4}.

(40)

Choose now
\tilde{\epsilon}=\tilde{\epsilon}_{J-1}
according to Proposition
5.1
corresponding to
\epsilon/2
,
\delta/(4J)
, initial value
\tilde{\phi}(t_{J-1})
and time-horizon
\Delta
. Using the Markov property of
Z^{N}
, we compute

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,t_{J-1}]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\tilde{\phi}(t)|<\tilde{\epsilon}\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\cdot\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\inf_{z\in A;|z-\tilde{\phi}(t_{J-1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[t_{J-1},T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\tilde{\phi}(t)|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big];

here, we denote (by a slight abuse of notation) the process starting at
z
at time
t_{J-1}
by
Z^{N,z}
.
Proposition
5.1
implies

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,t_{J-1}]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\tilde{\phi}(t)|<\tilde{\epsilon}_{J-1}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\Big(\inf_{z\in A;|z-\tilde{\phi}(t_{J-1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[t_{J-1},T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\tilde{\phi}(t)|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,t_{J-1}]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\tilde{\phi}(t)|<\tilde{\epsilon}_{J-1}\Big]-\int_{t_{J-1}}^{T}\ell(\tilde{\phi}(t),\mu^{J})dt-\frac{\delta}{4J}.

Iterating this procedure, we obtain

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\geq-\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}}^{t_{r}}\ell(\tilde{\phi}(t),\mu^{r})dt-\frac{\delta}{4}

and the assertion follows from Inequality (
40
) if we let
\delta\rightarrow 0
.

We note that the convergence is uniform in
x
by the uniformity in Proposition
5.1
.
∎

Theorem 5.3
.

Assume that
\log\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) is bounded and continuous.
Let
G\subset D([0,T];A)
be open and
x\in A
. Then,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}[Z^{N,x}\in G]\geq-\inf_{\phi\in G,}I_{T,x}(\phi).

The convergence is uniform in
x\in A
.

Proof.

Let
\inf_{\phi\in G}I_{T,x}(\phi)=:I^{*}<\infty
; hence, for
\delta>0
, there exists a
\phi^{\delta}\in G
(
\phi(0)=x
) with

I_{T,x}(\phi^{\delta})\leq I^{*}+\delta
. For small enough
\epsilon=\epsilon(\phi^{\delta})>0
, we have

\Big\{\phi\in D([0,T];A)|\sup_{t\in[0,T]};|\phi^{\delta}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big\}\subset G

and therefore

\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\phi^{\delta}(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\leq\mathbb{P}[Z^{N,x}\in G].

This implies by Theorem
5.2
that for all
\delta>0
,

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}[Z^{N,x}\in G]

\displaystyle\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x}(t)-\phi^{\delta}(t)|<\epsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\geq-I_{T,x}(\phi^{\delta})

\displaystyle\geq-I^{*}-\delta.

This implies

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}[Z^{N,x}\in G]\geq-I^{*}

as desired.
∎

We obtain the following result.

Corollary 5.4
.

Assume that
\log\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) is bounded and continuous. Then for all
\phi\in D([0,T];A)
with
\phi(0)=x
and
\epsilon,\delta>0
, there exists an
\tilde{\epsilon}>0
such that

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\Big(\inf_{z\in A;|x-z|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,z}-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\Big)\geq-I_{T,x}(\phi)-\delta.

Proof.

We assume w.l.o.g. that
I_{T,x}(\phi)<\infty
. By Theorem
5.3
, there exists an
N_{0}
and
\tilde{\epsilon}
such that for
N\geq N_{0}
and
z
with
|z-x|<\tilde{\epsilon}
,

\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,z}-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\geq-\inf_{\tilde{\phi}:\|\phi-\tilde{\phi}\|<\epsilon}I_{T,x}(\tilde{\phi})-\delta\geq-I_{T,x}(\phi)-\delta.

The assertion follows.
∎

5.2
LDP lower bound with vanishing rates

In the following, we drop the assumption that the log-rates are bounded. Instead, we rather consider situations, where Assumption
2.3
is satisfied.

We start by some preliminary considerations and assume that Assumption
2.3
(A1) and (A2) are satisfied. We note that there exists a constant
\alpha>0
such that for all
x\in A
there exists a
i\leq I
such that
B(x,\alpha)\subset B_{i}
. Indeed, assume that this is incorrect and consider a sequence of points
x_{n}\in A
such that
B(x_{n},1/n)
is not contained in any
B_{i}
. W.l.o.g., we can assume that
x_{n}\rightarrow x\in A
(recall that
A
is compact). As
x\in B_{i_{0}}
for some
i_{0}
, we have
B(x_{n},1/n)\subset B_{i_{0}}
for
n
large enough, a contradiction.

Lemma 5.5
.

Assume that
\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) is bounded and that Assumption
2.3
(A1) and (A2) are satisfied. Then, for
T>0
,
K>0
, there exists a
J=J(T,K)\in\mathds{N}
such that for all
\phi\in D([0,T];A)
with
I_{T}(\phi)\leq K
, there exist

0=t_{0}<t_{1}<\cdots<t_{J}=T\text{ and }i_{1},\dots,i_{J}\text{ such that }\phi(t)\in B_{i_{r}}\text{ for }t\in[t_{r-1},t_{r}].

Furthermore, for
r=1,\dots,J
,

\dist(\phi(t_{r-1}),\partial B_{i_{r}})\geq\alpha\quad\text{and}\quad\dist(\phi(t),\partial B_{i_{r}})\geq\alpha/2\quad\text{for }t\in[t_{r-1},t_{r}]

for
\alpha
as before.

Proof.

By the considerations above, we have
B(x,\alpha)\subset B_{i_{1}}
for an appropriate
i_{1}
. We define

\tilde{t}_{1}:=\inf\{t\geq 0|B(\phi(t),\alpha/2)\not\subset B_{i_{1}}\}\wedge T>0.

Now, there exists an
i_{2}
such that
B(\phi(t_{1}),\alpha)\subset B_{i_{2}}
. If
\tilde{t}_{1}<T
, we define

\tilde{t}_{2}:=\inf\{t\geq t_{1}|B(\phi(t),\alpha/2)\not\subset B_{i_{2}}\}\wedge T>\tilde{t}_{1}.

In the same way, we proceed. By the uniform absolute continuity (Lemma
4.17
) of all
\phi
with
I_{T}(\phi)\leq K
, we have

\tilde{t}_{r}-\tilde{t}_{r-1}\geq\delta\quad\text{for a constant }\delta>0\text{ independent of }\phi.

The assertion hence follows for
J:=\lfloor\frac{T}{\delta}\rfloor+1
and
t_{r}:=r\delta
(
r=1,\dots,J-1
),
t_{J}:=T
.
∎

We now define a function
\phi^{\eta}
which is close to a given function
\phi
with
I_{T}(\phi)<\infty
. We assume that Assumption
2.3
(A) holds. Hence, for
x\in B_{i}\cap A
and
t\in(0,\lambda_{2})
,

\di(x+tv_{i},\partial A)>\lambda_{1}t.

Note that
\lambda_{1}\leq 1
. Let
\eta>0
be small. We define for
r=1,\dots,J
, with the notation
\sum_{j=1}^{0}...=0
,

\eta_{r}:=\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\big)^{j-1}.

—

For
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1},t_{r-1}+\eta_{r}\big]
,

\phi^{\eta}(t):=\phi(t_{r-1})+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}v_{i_{j}}+\big(t-t_{r-1}-\eta_{r-1}\big)v_{i_{r}}.

—

For
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r},t_{r}+\eta_{r}\big]
,

\phi^{\eta}(t):=\phi\big(t-\eta_{r}\big)+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}v_{i_{j}}.

We make the following assumptions on
\eta
:

\eta_{J}=\eta\sum_{r=1}^{J}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{r-1}\leq\frac{\alpha}{4}\wedge\min_{r=1,\dots,J}|t_{r}-t_{r-1}|\wedge\lambda_{2}.

Therefore, we have the following properties for
\phi^{\eta}
:

—

For
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1},t_{r-1}+\eta_{r}\big]
,

|\phi(t)-\phi^{\eta}(t)|\leq|\phi(t)-\phi(t_{r-1})|+\eta_{r-1}+(\eta_{r}-\eta_{r-1})=V_{t_{r}-t_{r-1}}(\phi)+\eta_{r}\rightarrow 0

(41)

as
\eta\rightarrow 0
, where
V_{\cdot}(\phi)
is the modulus of continuity of
\phi
.
Similarly, for
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r},t_{r}+\eta_{r}\big]
,

|\phi(t)-\phi^{\eta}(t)|\leq|\phi(t)-\phi(t-\eta_{r})|+\eta_{r}=V_{\eta_{r}}(\phi)+\eta_{r}\rightarrow 0

as
\eta\rightarrow 0
.

—

For
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1},t_{r-1}+\eta_{r}\big]
,

\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial B_{i_{r}})\geq\dist(\phi(t_{r-1}),\partial B_{i_{r}})-\eta_{r}\geq\alpha-\frac{\alpha}{4}.

Similarly, for
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r},t_{r}+\eta_{r}\big]
, hence
t-\eta_{r}\in[t_{r-1},t_{r}]
,

\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial B_{i_{r}})\geq\dist(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\partial B_{i_{r}})-\eta_{r}\geq\frac{\alpha}{2}-\frac{\alpha}{4}.

Hence, for
r=1,\dots,J
,
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1},t_{r}+\eta_{r}\big]
,

\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial B_{i_{r}})\geq\frac{\alpha}{4}.

—

For
t\in[0,\eta]
,

\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial A)\geq t\lambda_{1}.

(42)

For
t\in\big[\eta,T+\eta_{J}\big]
,

\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial A)\geq\lambda_{1}\eta.

(43)

This can be seen by induction on
r=1,\dots,J
(the induction hypothesis is clear, cf. Inequality (
42
)). For
r=1,\dots,J
, we have (by induction hypothesis and the assumptions on
\eta
)

\phi^{\eta}\Big(t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1}\Big)\in B_{i_{r}},\text{ and for }r\geq 2,\ \dist\Big(\phi^{\eta}\Big(t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1}\Big),\partial A\Big)\geq\eta\lambda_{1}.

From Assumption
2.3
(A3), the distance of
\phi^{\eta}(t)
to the boundary is increasing for
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\big)^{j-1},t_{r-1}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\big)^{j-1}\big]
, and is at least

\Big(t-\Big(t_{r-1}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}\Big)\Big)\cdot\lambda_{1}\vee\eta\lambda_{1}.

In particular,

\dist\Big(\phi^{\eta}\Big(t_{r-1}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}\Big),\partial A\Big)\geq\eta\lambda_{1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{r-1}.

For
t\in\big[t_{r-1}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\big)^{j-1},t_{r}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\big)^{j-1}\big]
,
we have

\displaystyle\phi^{\eta}(t)

\displaystyle=\phi\Big(t-\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}\Big)+\eta\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{r-1}v_{i_{r}}+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}v_{i_{j}}

\displaystyle=\bar{\phi}(t)+\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}v_{i_{j}}

and therefore (by elementary calculus and the fact that
|v_{i}|\leq 1
)

\displaystyle\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial A)

\displaystyle\geq\dist(\bar{\phi}(t),\partial A)-\Big|\eta\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}v_{i_{j}}\Big|

\displaystyle\geq\eta\left(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\right)^{r-1}\lambda_{1}\left(1-\frac{{\bf 1}_{r\geq 2}}{2}\right)

\displaystyle\geq\eta\lambda_{1}.

We now have

Lemma 5.6
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3
holds. Let
K>0
and
\epsilon>0
. Then there exists an
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(T,K,\epsilon)>0
such that for all
\phi\in D([0,T];A)
with
I_{T}(\phi)\leq K
and all
\eta<\eta_{0}
,

I_{T}(\phi^{\eta})\leq I_{T}(\phi)+\epsilon,

where
\phi^{\eta}(t)
is defined as above.

Proof.

We first use Lemma
2.4
and chose
\eta<\eta_{1}
small enough (independent of
i
,
\phi
) such that

\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}+\eta_{r-1}}^{t_{r-1}+\eta_{r}}L(\phi^{\eta}(t),(\phi^{\eta})^{\prime}(t))dt=\sum_{r=1}^{J}I_{\eta_{r}-\eta_{r-1}}(\phi)<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

We now denote by
\mu^{*}(t)
the optimal
\mu
corresponding to
(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))
(cf. Lemma
4.5
). We let
r=1,\dots,J
and
t\in[t_{r-1}+\eta_{r},t_{r}+\eta_{r}]
and note that
(\phi^{\eta})^{\prime}(t)=\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})
. By Theorem
4.9
, we have

L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))\leq\ell(\phi^{\eta}(t),\mu^{*}(t-\eta_{r})).

(44)

By the Lipschitz continuity of the
\beta_{j}
, we have

|\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))-\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))|\leq\delta_{K}(\eta)

(45)

where
\delta_{K}(\eta)
is independent of
\phi
and
\delta_{K}(\eta)\rightarrow 0
as
\eta\rightarrow 0
. We deduce
from (
44
) and (
45
)

\displaystyle L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))

\displaystyle\leq k\delta_{K}(\eta)+\sum_{j}\mu_{j}^{*}(t-\eta_{r})\log\frac{\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))}{\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))}.

(46)

Let

\tilde{v}_{i_{r}}=\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{r-1}v_{i_{r}}+\sum_{j=1}^{r-1}\Big(\frac{3}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)^{j-1}v_{i_{j}},\ \text{ and }\hat{v}_{i_{r}}=\frac{\tilde{v}_{i_{r}}}{|\tilde{v}_{i_{r}}|}\in\mathcal{C}_{1,i_{r}}.

By Assumption
2.3
(B4), there exists a constant
\lambda_{4}>0
such that for
z\in B_{i_{r}}
and
\eta<\eta_{2}\leq\eta_{1}
small enough (note that
\eta_{2}
depends on
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
but not directly on
\phi
, except through
K
),

\beta_{j}(z)<\lambda_{4}\Rightarrow\beta_{j}(z+\eta\tilde{v}_{i_{r}})\geq\beta_{j}(z),

hence

\log\frac{\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))}{\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))}<0\quad\text{if }\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))<\lambda_{4}.

(47)

If
\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))\geq\lambda_{4}
(recall the definition of
\delta_{K}(\eta)
and choose
\eta<\eta_{3}<\eta_{2}
small enough such that

\delta_{K}(\eta)<\lambda_{4}/2
)

\displaystyle\log\frac{\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))}{\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))}

\displaystyle\leq\log\frac{\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))}{\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))-\delta_{K}(\eta)}

\displaystyle\leq\log\frac{\lambda_{4}}{\lambda_{4}-\delta_{K}(\eta)}

\displaystyle=\log\frac{1}{1-\delta_{K}(\eta)/\lambda_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)}{\lambda_{4}},

(48)

since
\log(1/(1-x))<2x
for
0<x\leq 1/2
.

From Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.5
, there exist (universal, i.e., independent of
x
) constants
B\geq B_{1}\vee B_{2}
,
B>1
,
C_{1}
,
C_{3}
such that for
|y|\geq B
, and
x\in A
,

L(x,y)\geq C_{1}|y|\log|y|,

(49)

|\mu^{*}|=|\mu^{*}(x,y)|\leq C_{3}|y|.

(50)

Hence if
|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|\geq B
, using (
46
), (
47
), (
48
) and (
50
) for the first inequality and
(
49
) for the second, we get

\displaystyle L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))

\displaystyle\leq k\delta_{K}(\eta)+kC_{3}|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)}{\lambda_{4}}

\displaystyle\leq k\delta_{K}(\eta)+kC_{3}\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))}{C_{1}\lambda_{4}\log|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|}.

(51)

If however
|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|<B
, Lemma
4.5
implies similarly as before that

|\mu^{*}(t-\eta_{r})|\leq C_{3}B
. From (
46
), we deduce

L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))\leq k\delta_{K}(\eta)+kC_{3}B\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)}{\lambda_{4}}.

(52)

Inequalities (
51
) and (
52
) imply

\displaystyle L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))\leq\delta_{1,K}(\eta)+\delta_{2,K}(\eta)L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))

with constants
\delta_{i,K}(\eta)\rightarrow 0
as
\eta\rightarrow 0
. We can hence choose
\eta<\eta_{4}<\eta_{3}
small enough such that

\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}+\eta_{r}}^{t_{r}+\eta_{r}}L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))dt-\sum_{r=1}^{J}\int_{t_{r-1}+\eta_{r}}^{t_{r}+\eta_{r}}L(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))dt<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

This yields the result.
∎

The following lemma is the main difference to the corresponding result of
Shwartz and Weiss 2005
. We transform the LLN from Assumption
2.3
(C) to a LDP lower bound for linear functions following the vector
v_{i}
near the boundary.

In the next statement,
\alpha
is the exponent which appears in the Assumption
2.3
(C).

Lemma 5.7
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3
holds. Let
i\leq I_{1}
,
x\in A\cap B_{i}

and
x^{N}\in A^{N}\cap B_{i}
such that

\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}|x^{N}-x|N^{\alpha}<1.

Let furthermore
\epsilon>0
and define
\mu^{i}
,
\phi^{x}
and
\eta_{0}
as in Assumption
2.3
(C). Then for all
\eta
small enough,
all
\epsilon
,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi^{x}(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\geq-\int_{0}^{\eta}\ell(\phi^{x}(t),\mu^{i})dt,

and the above convergence is uniform in
x\in A
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same line of reasoning as the proof of Proposition
5.1
but is technically more involved.

For simplicity, let
N
be large enough and
\eta<\eta_{0}
(for
\eta_{0}
as in Assumption
2.3
(C)) be small enough such that
\phi^{x^{N}}(t)\in B_{i}
for all
t\leq\eta
. We furthermore let

\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{1}:=\epsilon\wedge\lambda_{1}\eta

Define the set

F^{N}:=\Big\{\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi^{x}(t)|<\tilde{\epsilon}\Big\}.

Let
\xi_{\eta}=\xi^{N}_{\eta}=\frac{d\tilde{\mathbb{P}}}{d\mathbb{P}}|_{\mathcal{F}_{\eta}}
be given as in Theorem
A.1
for the rates
\tilde{\beta}_{j}=\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}
.We note that due to Assumption
2.3
(C),

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]\geq 1-\delta(N,\tilde{\epsilon})\rightarrow 1\quad\text{as }N\rightarrow\infty.

(53)

From Corollary
A.2
, (
53
) and Jensen’s inequality, we deduce that

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi^{x}(t)|<\epsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\big[F^{N}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\xi_{\eta}^{-1}\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\Big\{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\big[F^{N}\big]\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{F^{N}}\big[\exp(X_{\eta})\big]\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\big[F^{N}\big]+\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{F^{N}}\big[\exp(X_{\eta})\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{F^{N}}\Big[\frac{X_{\eta}}{N}\Big],

(54)

where
\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{F^{N}}[X]:=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[X|F^{N}]

and

\displaystyle X_{\eta}^{N}:=X_{\eta}:=\log\xi_{\eta}^{-1}

\displaystyle=\sum_{\tau\leq\eta}\Big[\log\beta_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau-)-\log\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau-)\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad+N\sum_{j}\int_{0}^{\eta}\big(\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))\big)dt.

We have
\dist(\phi^{x^{N}}(t),\partial A)\geq\lambda_{1}t
(cf. Assumption
2.3
(A3)) and therefore on
F^{N}
,

\dist(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t),\partial A)>\lambda_{1}t-\tilde{\epsilon}\quad\text{for }t\in\Big[\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}},\eta\Big].

Consequently

\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))=\mu^{i}_{j}\quad\text{for all }j\text{ and for all }t\in\Big[\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}},\eta\Big].

We obtain

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\int_{0}^{\eta}\sum_{j}\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))dt\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\Big(\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\int_{\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}^{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))dt\Big]+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\int_{0}^{\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))dt\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\qquad=:\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}^{\eta}\mu^{i}_{j}dt+X_{1}^{N}(\tilde{\epsilon}),

(55)

since
\mu^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))=\mu^{i}_{j}
on
F^{N}
.
We note that for all
N
,

|X_{1}^{N}(\tilde{\epsilon})|\leq\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}k\bar{\mu}\quad\text{where }\bar{\mu}:=\max_{j=1,\dots,k}\mu^{i}_{j}.

(56)

By the fact that the
\beta_{j}
’s are bounded and continuous and by Theorem
3.1
, we have for
j=1,\dots,k
,

\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))-\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))|\rightarrow 0\quad\text{a.s.\penalty\ as }N\rightarrow\infty.

Combined with (
53
), this implies

\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\int_{0}^{\eta}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))dt\Big]\longrightarrow\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{0}^{\eta}\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))dt

(57)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
.

Let us now define the following processes. For
z\in A
,
j=1,\dots,k
and
0\leq s<t\leq\eta
let

\bar{Z}^{N,z}
solves Equation (
3
) with constant rates
\mu^{i}_{j}
under
\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
, and

\displaystyle Y_{j}^{N,z,s,t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\cdot\#\text{jumps of }Z^{N,z}\text{ in direction }h_{j}\text{ in }[s,t],

\displaystyle\bar{Y}_{j}^{N,z,s,t}

\displaystyle:=\frac{1}{N}\cdot\#\text{jumps of }\bar{Z}^{N,z}\text{ in direction }h_{j}\text{ in }[s,t],

We have for any event
F
, noting that

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F]-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F]^{2}=\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{c}]-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{c}]^{2}
,

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,z,s,t}]

\displaystyle\leq(t-s)\mu^{i}_{j}=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\bar{Y}_{j}^{N,z,s,t}],

(58)

\displaystyle\widetilde{\Var}[Y_{j}^{N,z,s,t}]

\displaystyle\leq(t-s)\mu^{i}_{j}=\widetilde{\Var}[\bar{Y}_{j}^{N,z,s,t}],

(59)

\displaystyle|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F},Y_{j}^{N,z,s,t})|,\,|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F},\bar{Y}_{j}^{N,z,s,t})|

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\widetilde{\Var}[\mathds{1}_{F}]}\sqrt{\widetilde{\Var}[\bar{Y}_{j}^{N,z,s,t}]}

\displaystyle\leq\sqrt{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F]-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F]^{2}}\sqrt{(t-s)\mu^{i}_{j}}.

(60)

We define the sets

F_{1}^{N}:=\Big\{\Big|Z^{N,x^{N}}\Big(\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)-\phi^{x}\Big(\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)\Big|<\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}\Big\}\in\mathcal{F}_{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}

and for
z\in A
with
|z-\phi^{x}(2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2
,

F_{2}^{N,z}:=\Big\{\sup_{t\in[0,\eta-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\phi^{z}(t)|<\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}\Big\}.

Note that

\dist(\phi^{x}(t),\partial A)\geq 2\tilde{\epsilon}\quad\text{for }t\in\Big[\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}},\eta\Big]

and whenever
|z-\phi^{x}(2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2
,

\Big|\phi^{z}(t)-\phi^{x}\Big(t+\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}\Big)\Big|<\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}\quad\text{for }t\in\Big[0,\eta-\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}\Big].

Hence

\dist(Z^{N,z}(t),\partial A)\geq\tilde{\epsilon}\quad\text{for }t\in\Big[0,\eta-\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}\Big]

and therefore
Z^{N,z}(t)=\bar{Z}^{N,z}(t)
on
F_{2}^{N,z}
. This implies

F^{N,z}_{2}=\Big\{\sup_{t\in[0,\eta-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}]}|\bar{Z}^{N,z}(t)-\phi^{z}(t)|<\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{2}\Big\}.

(61)

We now let

\frac{2\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}\leq s<t\leq\eta

and compute (by using the Markov property of
Z^{N}
and the fact that
Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}=\bar{Y}_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}
on the event
F_{2}^{N,z}
)

\displaystyle\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]

\displaystyle=\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{F^{N}_{1}}Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{(F^{N}_{1})^{c}}Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}]+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{(F^{N}_{1})^{c}}Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}_{2}}Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{(F^{N,z}_{2})^{c}}Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{(F^{N}_{1})^{c}}Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]

\displaystyle=\mu_{j}^{i}(t-s)\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}_{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}_{2}},Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}})

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{(F^{N,z}_{2})^{c}}Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}]

\displaystyle\qquad+\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{(F^{N}_{1})^{c}}Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]

(62)

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{j}^{i}(t-s)\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}_{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\big|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}_{2}},Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}})\big|

(63)

as the third and the fourth term in (
62
) are non-negative.
As furthermore

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}_{2}]\rightarrow 1

and

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}_{2}},Y_{j}^{N,z,s-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}})\rightarrow 0

as
N\rightarrow\infty
by Assumption
2.3
(C), Theorem
3.1
and (
60
). Combinig the resulting
inequality with (
58
) for all
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{1}
and
2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}\leq s<t
, we deduce that

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]=\mu^{i}_{j}(t-s).

Note that for
0\leq s<t\leq\eta
, and all
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{1}

Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}=Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,(s\vee 2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})\wedge t}+Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},(s\vee 2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})\wedge t,t}

and hence also for
0\leq s<t\leq\eta
, since when
s<2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}
,
Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}\wedge t}
is of the order of

\tilde{\epsilon}
,

\lim_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},s,t}]=\mu^{i}_{j}(t-s).

(64)

Let now
\tau_{j}\in[0,\eta]
denote the jump times of
Z^{N,x^{N}}
in direction
h_{j}
. Since

\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))=\log\mu^{i}_{j}

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
a.s.,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j;\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\leq\eta}\log\tilde{\mu}^{i}_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j;\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\log\mu^{i}_{j}\,\,\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}Y^{N,x^{N},0,\eta}_{j}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j;\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\log\mu^{i}_{j}\Big\{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]\cdot\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[Y^{N,x^{N},0,\eta}_{j}\big]+\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N}},Y^{N,x^{N},0,\eta}_{j})\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\longrightarrow\sum_{j;\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\eta\mu^{i}_{j}\log\mu^{i}_{j}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}\int_{0}^{\eta}\mu^{i}_{j}\log\mu^{i}_{j}dt

(65)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
by (
53
), (
60
) and (
64
).

For the last and most extensive step of the proof, we define for
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{1}
and (cf. Assumption
2.3
(C) and (
11
))

\epsilon_{N}=\frac{1}{N^{\alpha}}

and the set

\tilde{F}^{N}:=\Big\{\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi^{x}(t)|<\epsilon_{N}\Big\}.

We assume w.l.o.g. that from now on
N
is large enough (cf. Assumption
2.3
(C)) such that

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\big[\tilde{F}^{N}\big]\geq\tilde{\mathbb{P}}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi^{x^{N}}(t)|<\epsilon_{N}\Big]\geq 1-\delta(N,\epsilon_{N}),

where
\delta(N,\epsilon_{N})\to 0

as
N\rightarrow\infty
. We note that we have for all
\tilde{\epsilon}\leq\epsilon_{1}
,
j=1,\dots,k
with
\mu^{i}_{j}>0
,
N\in\mathds{N}
and
t\in[2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta]
,

\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t)),\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(t))\geq\log\underline{\beta}(\tilde{\epsilon})>0\quad\text{on }F^{N}\text{ and }\tilde{F}^{N}.

We compute

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\sum_{\tau\leq\eta}\log\beta_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[0,2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[0,\eta]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big].

(66)

Let us first consider the first term in Equation (
66
). As for all
j
,

\log\beta_{j}(\cdot):\tilde{A}(\tilde{\epsilon}):=\{z\in A|\dist(z,\partial A)\geq\tilde{\epsilon}\}\rightarrow\mathds{R}

is uniformly continuous, there exit constants
\tilde{\delta}_{N}>0
with
\tilde{\delta}_{N}\downarrow 0
such that

z,\tilde{z}\in\tilde{A}(\tilde{\epsilon}),\,|\tilde{z}-z|<3\epsilon_{N}\Rightarrow|\log\beta_{j}(\tilde{z})-\log\beta_{j}(z)|<\tilde{\delta}_{N}.

(67)

We define

M=M(N):=\lfloor(\eta-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})\epsilon_{N}^{-1}+1\rfloor

and divide the interval
[2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta]
into
M
equidistant subintervals
[t_{r},t_{r+1}]
(
r=0,\dots,M-1
,
t_{r}=t_{r}(N)
) of length
\Delta=\Delta(N)
, i.e. (for
N
large enough),

\frac{\epsilon_{N}}{2}\leq\Delta<\epsilon_{N}.

For
j=1,\dots,k
,
r=0,\dots,M-1
and
\tau_{j},t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]
we have,

|Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-)-\phi^{x}(t)|\leq 2\epsilon_{N}\quad\text{on }\tilde{F}^{N},

since
|\phi^{x}(\tau_{j})-\phi^{x}(t)|\leq|\tau_{j}-t|
as
|v_{i}|\leq 1
, and hence (cf. (
67
))

\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))-\tilde{\delta}_{N}\leq\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-)).

From this inequality, we deduce

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau\in[2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta]}\log\beta_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\Big(\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))-\tilde{\delta}_{N}\Big)\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}Y^{N,x^{N},t_{r},t_{r+1}}_{j}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[Y^{N,x^{N},t_{r},t_{r+1}}_{j}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\big|\log\underline{\beta}(\tilde{\epsilon})\big|\big|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}},Y^{N,x^{N},t_{r},t_{r+1}}_{j})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\delta}_{N}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}Y^{N,x^{N},2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta}].

(68)

The second term in Inequality (
68
) satisfies (cf. Inequality (
60
) and Assumption
2.3
(C); we assume that
N
is sufficiently large such that
M\leq 2\epsilon_{N}^{-1}\eta
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\big|\log\underline{\beta}(\tilde{\epsilon})\big|\big|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}},Y^{N,x^{N},t_{r},t_{r+1}}_{j})\big|

\displaystyle\qquad\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}2k\eta\big|\log\underline{\beta}(\tilde{\epsilon})\big|\epsilon_{N}^{-1}\sqrt{\bar{\mu}\;\epsilon_{N}}\sqrt{\delta(N,\epsilon_{N})}

\displaystyle\qquad\rightarrow 0

(69)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
. The third term in Equation (
68
) satisfies

\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\delta}_{N}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}Y^{N,x^{N},2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta}]\leq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\delta}_{N}k\bar{\mu}\eta\rightarrow 0

(70)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Finally, let us consider the first term in Equation (
68
). Recall that by (
58
) and (
63
), we have

\displaystyle\mu_{j}^{i}(t_{r+1}-t_{r})

\displaystyle\geq\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},t_{r},t_{r+1}}]

\displaystyle\geq\mu_{j}^{i}(t_{r+1}-t_{r})\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}_{2}]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\big|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}_{2}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r}-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t_{r+1}-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}})\big|.

We define

\displaystyle\alpha_{1}^{N}

\displaystyle:=\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]},

\displaystyle\alpha^{N}_{2}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N,z}_{2}]<1,

\displaystyle\alpha_{3}^{N}

\displaystyle:=\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\cdot\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\big|\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{F^{N,z}_{2}},Y_{j}^{N,z,t_{r}-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},t_{r+1}-2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}})\big|,

\displaystyle\phi^{r}_{j}

\displaystyle:=\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t)),

\displaystyle S^{N}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\mu^{i}_{j}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}(t_{r+1}-t_{r})\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t)).

We compute (for
N
large enough as before)

\displaystyle\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\big[Y^{N,x^{N},t_{r},t_{r+1}}_{j}\big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\alpha_{1}^{N}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))\cdot\Big\{\mathds{1}_{\{\phi^{r}_{j}<0\}}\mu_{j}(t_{r+1}-t_{r})

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\mathds{1}_{\{\phi^{r}_{j}>0\}}\big(\alpha_{2}^{N}\mu_{j}(t_{r+1}-t_{r})-\alpha_{3}^{N}\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\alpha_{1}^{N}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\mu_{j}^{i}\sum_{r=0}^{M-1}(t_{r+1}-t_{r})\inf_{t\in[t_{r},t_{r+1})}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))\Big\{\mathds{1}_{\{\phi^{r}_{j}<0\}}+\alpha_{2}^{N}\mathds{1}_{\{\phi^{r}_{j}>0\}}\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad-2\eta\alpha_{1}^{N}k|\log\bar{\beta}|\epsilon_{N}^{-1}\alpha_{3}^{N}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\alpha_{1}^{N}S^{N}-2\eta\alpha_{1}^{N}k|\log\bar{\beta}|\epsilon_{N}^{-1}\alpha_{3}^{N}-\alpha_{1}^{N}k|\log\bar{\beta}|\bar{\mu}\eta(1-\alpha_{2}^{N}).

(71)

We readily observe that

\alpha_{1}^{N},\alpha_{2}^{N}\rightarrow 1\quad\text{as }N\rightarrow\infty

(72)

by Theorem
3.1
and Assumption
2.3
(C). We furthermore note that by Assumption
2.3
(C) and Theorem
3.1
(cf. also the comment corresponding to (
10
) and again the fact that the rate of convergence in Theorem
3.1
is independent of initial values),

\frac{\inf_{z}\sqrt{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F_{2}^{N,z}]-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F_{2}^{N,z}]^{2}}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{N}}}\leq\frac{\inf_{z}\sqrt{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[(F_{2}^{N,z})^{c}]}}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{N}}}\rightarrow 0\quad\text{as }N\rightarrow\infty.

Therefore (for
N
sufficiently large as before),

\displaystyle 2\eta\alpha_{1}^{N}k|\log\bar{\beta}|\epsilon_{N}^{-1}\alpha_{3}^{N}

\displaystyle\leq 2\eta\alpha_{1}^{N}k|\log\bar{\beta}|\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}_{1}]\sqrt{\bar{\mu}}\frac{1}{\sqrt{\epsilon_{N}}}\inf_{z;|z-\phi^{x}(\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1})|<\tilde{\epsilon}/2}\sqrt{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F_{2}^{N,z}]-\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F_{2}^{N,z}]^{2}}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0

(73)

as
N\rightarrow 0
. Finally,
S^{N}
is a Riemann sum and we have

S^{N}\rightarrow\sum_{j}\mu^{i}_{j}\int_{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}^{\eta}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))dt\quad\text{as }N\rightarrow\infty.

(74)

We observe that (
68
) - (
74
) yield

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1},\eta]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mu^{i}_{j}\int_{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}^{\eta}\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))dt.

(75)

We now consider the second term in the right ahnd side of (
66
). We define

\tilde{M}=\tilde{M}(N):=\lfloor 2\tilde{\epsilon}\epsilon_{N}^{-1}+1\rfloor

and divide the interval
[0,2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}]
into
\tilde{M}
subintervals
[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]
(
r=0,\dots,\tilde{M}-1
,
\tilde{t}_{r}=\tilde{t}_{r}(N)
) of length
\tilde{\Delta}=\tilde{\Delta}(N)
, i.e., for
N
large enough,

\frac{\epsilon_{N}}{2\lambda_{1}}\leq\tilde{\Delta}<\frac{\epsilon_{N}}{\lambda_{1}}.

For
r=0,\dots,\tilde{M}-1
and
\tau_{j}\in[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]
, we obtain on
\tilde{F}^{N}
,

\displaystyle\dist(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-),\partial A)

\displaystyle>\dist(\phi^{x}(\tau_{j}-),\partial A)-2\epsilon_{N}

\displaystyle\geq\dist(\phi^{x}(\tilde{t}_{r}),\partial A)-2\epsilon_{N}

\displaystyle\geq\lambda_{1}\tilde{t}_{r}-2\epsilon_{N}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{r-4}{2}\epsilon_{N}.

(76)

Hence,
\dist(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-),\partial A)>\epsilon_{N}
for
r\geq 6
. We compute for
j
with
\mu_{j}>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[0,2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=0}^{\tilde{M}-1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad=\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=6}^{\tilde{M}-1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=0}^{5}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big].

(77)

We note that for all
j
,
\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\geq\underline{\beta}(\lambda_{0}/N)

\tilde{\mathbb{P}}
-a.s. by Assumption
2.3
(A1).
The second term in the right hand side of (
77
) can be bounded from below (w.l.o.g.
\underline{\beta}(\lambda_{0}/N)<1
):

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=0}^{5}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{4}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\log\underline{\beta}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{N}\Big)\sum_{r=0}^{5}\Big\{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,x^{N},\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}}]+\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}},Y_{j}^{N,z,\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}})\Big\}

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{6\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\log\underline{\beta}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{N}\Big)\bar{\mu}\frac{\epsilon_{N}}{\lambda_{1}}+\frac{4}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\log\underline{\beta}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{N}\Big)\sqrt{\frac{\bar{\mu}\;\epsilon_{N}}{\lambda_{1}}}\sqrt{\delta(N,\epsilon_{N})}

\displaystyle\qquad\rightarrow 0

(78)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
by Assumption
2.3
(C) (cf. also (
9
)). For the first term in Equation (
77
), we compute for
j
with
\mu_{j}>0
(similarly as before, we assume w.l.o.g. that
\underline{\beta}(\tilde{\epsilon})<1
and note that

\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\geq\underline{\beta}(\lambda_{1}\tilde{t}_{r}-\epsilon_{N})\geq\underline{\beta}((r-4)\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)
)

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=6}^{\tilde{M}-1}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\quad\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=2}^{\tilde{M}-5}\log\underline{\beta}(r\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}}Y^{N,x^{N},\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}}]

\displaystyle\quad=\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\mu_{j}^{i}\sum_{r=2}^{\tilde{M}-5}\tilde{\Delta}\log\underline{\beta}(r\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=2}^{\tilde{M}-5}\log\underline{\beta}(r\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}},Y^{N,x^{N},\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}}).

(79)

For the first term in Equation (
79
), we have by Assumption
2.3
(C) (in particular by the fact that the integral below converges, cf. (
12
))

\frac{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[\tilde{F}^{N}]}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\mu_{j}^{i}\sum_{r=2}^{\tilde{M}-5}\tilde{\Delta}\log\underline{\beta}(r\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)\rightarrow\mu_{j}^{i}\int_{0}^{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}\log\underline{\beta}(\lambda_{1}\rho/2)d\rho

(80)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
. Similarly, we obtain for the second term in Equation (
79
),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{r=2}^{\tilde{M}-5}\log\underline{\beta}(r\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{\tilde{F}^{N}},Y^{N,x^{N},\tilde{t}_{r},\tilde{t}_{r+1}})

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sqrt{\mu_{j}^{i}\frac{\delta(N,\epsilon_{N})}{\tilde{\Delta}}}\sum_{r=2}^{\tilde{M}-5}\tilde{\Delta}\log\underline{\beta}(r\lambda_{1}\tilde{\Delta}/2)

\displaystyle\qquad\rightarrow 0

(81)

as
N\rightarrow 0
by Assumption
2.3
(C) (cf. (
8
) and (
12
)).

Finally, we consider the third term in Equation (
66
). We obtain by Assumption (
2.3
) (C),

\displaystyle\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\sum_{j,\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N}\setminus\tilde{F}^{N}}\sum_{\tau_{j}\in[0,\eta]}\log\beta_{j}(Z^{N,z}(\tau_{j}-))\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\log\underline{\beta}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{N}\Big)\sum_{j;\,\mu^{i}_{j}>0}\big\{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[(\tilde{F}^{N})^{c}]\tilde{\mathbb{E}}[Y_{j}^{N,z,0,\eta}]+\widetilde{\Cov}(\mathds{1}_{(\tilde{F}^{N})^{c}},Y_{j}^{N,z,0,\eta})\big\}

\displaystyle\geq\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\log\underline{\beta}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{N}\Big)k\,\bar{\mu}\,\eta\,\delta(N,\epsilon_{N})+\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\log\underline{\beta}\Big(\frac{\lambda_{0}}{N}\Big)k\sqrt{\bar{\mu}\,\eta\,\delta(N,\epsilon_{N})}

\displaystyle\rightarrow 0

(82)

as
N\rightarrow\infty
similarly as before (cf. (
8
) and (
9
)).

We obtain by Equation (
66
) and (
75
), (
77
) - (
82
),

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{\tilde{\mathbb{P}}[F^{N}]}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}\Big[\frac{1}{N}\mathds{1}_{F^{N}}\sum_{\tau\leq\eta}\log\beta_{j(\tau)}(Z^{N,x^{N}}(\tau-))\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\geq\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mu^{i}_{j}\int_{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}^{\eta}|\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))|dt-k\bar{\mu}\int^{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}_{0}|\log\underline{\beta}(\lambda_{1}\rho/2)|d\rho.

(83)

We conclude by letting
\delta>0
and choosing
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{1}
small enough such that (cf. Equations (
55
), (
56
) and Inequality (
83
); note that we require the convergence of the integral in (
12
) of Assumption
2.3
(C) here)

\frac{\tilde{\epsilon}}{\lambda_{1}}k\bar{\mu},\,k\bar{\mu}\int_{0}^{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}|\log\beta_{j}(\phi^{x}(t))|dt,k\bar{\mu}\int^{2\tilde{\epsilon}/\lambda_{1}}_{0}|\log\underline{\beta}(\lambda_{1}\rho/2)|d\rho<\frac{\delta}{4}.

The assertion now follows from Inequality (
54
) and (
55
), (
56
), (
57
), (
65
) and (
83
):

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\tilde{\mathbb{E}}_{F^{N}}\Big[\frac{X_{\eta}}{N}\Big]\geq-\int_{0}^{\eta}\ell(\phi^{x}(t),\mu)dt-\delta.

The uniformity of the convergence follows from the fact that we have used only Assumption
2.3
(C) and Theorem
3.1
, where the convergences
are uniform in
x
.
∎

Again in the following result, the exponent
\alpha
is the one from Assumption
2.3
(C).

Theorem 5.8
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3
holds. Let
x\in A

and
x^{N}\in A^{N}
such that

\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}|x^{N}-x|N^{\alpha}<1.

Then, for
\phi\in D([0,T];A)
and
\epsilon>0
,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\geq-I_{T,x}(\phi).

Moreover the above convergence is uniform in
x\in A

Proof.

We can w.l.o.g. assume that
I_{T,x}(\phi)\leq K<\infty
. Let
\delta>0
and divide the interval
[0,T]
into
J
subintervals as before. We define the function
\phi^{\eta}
as before and choose
\eta_{1}
small enough such that for all
\eta<\eta_{1}
(cf. Lemma
5.6
),

\int_{\eta}^{T}L(\phi^{\eta}(t),(\phi^{\eta})^{\prime}(t))dt<\int_{0}^{T}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt+\frac{\delta}{3}.

(84)

We furthermore assume that
\eta<\eta_{1}
is such that

\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|\phi(t)-\phi^{\eta}(t)|<\frac{\epsilon}{4}.

Hence,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]\geq\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big].

From (
43
), for
t\geq\eta
,

\dist(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial A)\geq\eta\lambda_{1}
.
We define

\epsilon_{1}=\epsilon_{1}(\eta)=\frac{\epsilon}{2}\wedge\frac{\lambda_{1}\eta}{4},

\underline{\beta}^{\eta}:=\inf\Big\{\beta_{j}(z)\Big|\ 1\leq j\leq k,\ z\in A,\dist(z,\partial A)\geq\frac{\eta\lambda_{1}}{2}\Big\}>0

and

\tilde{\beta}^{\eta}_{j}(z):=\begin{cases}\beta_{j}(z)\vee\underline{\beta}^{\eta}&\text{ if }z\in A\\
\tilde{\beta}_{j}^{\eta}(\psi_{A}(z))&\text{ else,}\end{cases}

where the function
\psi_{A}
has been specified in Assumption (A4).
We denote by
\tilde{Z}^{N,z,\eta}
the process starting at
z
at time
\eta
with rates
\tilde{\beta}_{j}^{\eta}
. As the
\log\tilde{\beta}_{j}^{\eta}
are bounded, we have by Theorem
5.2
that there exists an

\epsilon_{2}=\epsilon_{2}(\eta)<\epsilon_{1}(\eta)

such that for all
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{2}
,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\Big(\inf_{|z-\phi^{\eta}(\eta)|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|\tilde{Z}^{N,z,\eta}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}\Big]\Big)\geq-\int_{\eta}^{T}\tilde{L}^{\eta}(\phi^{\eta}(t),(\phi^{\eta})^{\prime}(t))dt-\frac{\delta}{3},

where
\tilde{L}^{\eta}
denotes the Legendre transform corresponding to the rates
\tilde{\beta}_{j}^{\eta}
. We readily observe that for all
t\in[\eta,T]
,

\tilde{L}^{\eta}(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\eta}(t))=L(\phi^{\eta}(t),(\phi^{\eta})^{\prime}(t))

and that for
|z-\phi^{\eta}(\eta)|<\tilde{\epsilon}
, denoting by an abuse of notation
Z^{N,z}
the process starting from
z
at time
\eta
,

\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\phi^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}\Leftrightarrow\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|\tilde{Z}^{N,z,\eta}(t)-\phi^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}.

and hence

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\frac{\epsilon}{2}\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}\Big]

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|\tilde{Z}^{N,z,\eta}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}\Big]

consequently for
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\Big(\inf_{|z-\phi^{\eta}(\eta)|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}\Big]\Big)

\displaystyle\geq-\int_{\eta}^{T}L(\phi^{\eta}(t),(\phi^{\eta})^{\prime}(t))dt-\frac{\delta}{3}

\displaystyle\geq-\int_{0}^{T}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt-\frac{2\delta}{3},

where we have used (
84
) for the second inequality.
We use the Markov property of
Z^{N}
and obtain for
\tilde{\epsilon}<\epsilon_{2}

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\geq\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,\eta]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\tilde{\epsilon}\Big]

\displaystyle\qquad\cdot\inf_{|z-\phi^{\eta}(\eta)|<\tilde{\epsilon}}\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[\eta,T]}|Z^{N,z}(t)-\tilde{\phi}^{\eta}(t)|<\epsilon_{1}\Big].

Combining the last two inequalities with Lemma
5.7
, we deduce that (
i
being the index of the ball
B_{i}

to which the starting point
x
belongs)

\displaystyle\liminf_{N\to\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\Big[\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Z^{N,x^{N}}(t)-\phi(t)|<\epsilon\Big]

\displaystyle\geq-\int_{0}^{\eta}\ell(\phi^{x}(t),\mu^{i})dt-\int_{0}^{T}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt-\frac{2\delta}{3}

\displaystyle\geq-\int_{0}^{T}L(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt-\delta

thanks to Lemma
5.9
below, provided
\eta
is small enough. The result follows since
\delta>0
is arbitrary.
∎

Lemma 5.9
.

Let
x\in B_{i}
, where
i\leq I_{1}
, and suppose
\phi^{x}(t)=x+tv_{i}
. Let moreover
\mu^{i}
be such that

\sum_{j=1}^{k}\mu^{i}_{j}h_{j}=v_{i}
. Then, uniformly in
x
, as
t\to 0
,

\int_{0}^{t}\ell(\phi^{x}(s),\mu^{i})ds\to 0.

Proof.

Since according to Assumption (A3)
d(\phi^{x}(t),\partial A)\geq\lambda_{1}t
, the result follows from
(
9
) from Assumption (C).
∎

Theorem 5.10
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3
as well as the assumptions from Theorem
5.8
hold. Then for
any open set
G\subset D([0,T];A)
,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}\big[Z^{N,x^{N}}\in G\big]\geq-\inf_{\phi\in G}I_{T,x}(\phi).

Moreover the convergence is uniform in
x
.

Proof.

The proof follows the same line of reasoning as the proof of Theorem
5.3
.
∎

We will need the following stronger version. Recall the definition of
A^{N}
at the start of section
2
.

Theorem 5.11
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3

holds. Then for
any open set
G\subset D([0,T];A)
and any compact subset
K\subset A
,

\liminf_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\inf_{x\in K\cap A^{N}}\mathbb{P}\big[Z^{N,x}\in G\big]\geq-\sup_{x\in K}\inf_{\phi\in G}I_{T,x}(\phi).

Proof.

This follows readily from the uniformity in
x
of the convergence in Theorem
5.10
.
∎

6
LDP upper bound

We now prove the LDP upper bound. For reasons of readability, we split up the proof into four parts. In the first three parts, we prove the main auxiliary results required (Sections
6.1
-
6.3
). Finally, we prove the main results of the section in Section
6.4
.

In this section, whenever we consider the process
Z^{N,x}
, we will mean that the process
Z^{N}
is started from the nearest point to
x
on the grid
A^{N}

(see the beginning of section
2
for the definition of
A^{N}
).

6.1
Piecewise linear approximation

The goal of this section is to prove that
Z^{N,x}
is exponentially close to its piecewise linear approximation.
For
Z^{N,x}
, we define the piecewise linear interpolation
Y^{N,x}
. To this end, we divide
[0,T]
into
N
subintervals
[t_{j-1},t_{j}]
with
t_{j}=\frac{jT}{N}
,
j=1,\dots,N
. We define
t\in[t_{j-1},t_{j}]

Y^{N,x}_{t}=Z^{N,x}(t_{j-1})+\frac{t-t_{j-1}}{t_{j}-t_{j-1}}(Z^{N,x}(t_{j})-Z^{N,x}(t_{j-1})).

(85)

We prove that
Y^{N,x}
is exponentially close to
Z^{N,x}
.

Lemma 6.1
.

Assume that
\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) is bounded. Let
\delta>0
. Then

\limsup_{N\rightarrow\infty}\frac{1}{N}\log\mathbb{P}[\di(Y^{N,x},Z^{N,x})>\delta]=-\infty

uniformly in
x\in A
.

Proof.

For any
1\leq j\leq[N/T]
, we have the inclusion

\{\sup_{t\in[t_{j-1},t_{j}]}|Y^{N,x}_{t}-Z^{N,x}_{t}|\geq\delta\}\subset\{\sup_{t\in[t_{j-1},t_{j}]}|Z^{N,x}_{t}-Z^{N,x}_{t_{j-1}}|\geq\delta/2\}.

It then follows from Lemma
3.6
that for some positive constant
C
and for each
j
,

\mathbb{P}(\sup_{t\in[t_{j-1},t_{j}]}|Y^{N,x}_{t}-Z^{N,x}_{t}|\geq\delta)\leq\exp\left(-CN\delta\log(CN\delta)\right)).

Consequently

\displaystyle\mathbb{P}\left(\sup_{t\in[0,T]}|Y^{N,x}_{t}-Z^{N,x}_{t}|\geq\delta\right)

\displaystyle=\mathbb{P}\left(\bigcup_{j=1}^{[N/T]}\left\{\sup_{t\in[t_{j-1},t_{j}]}|Y^{N,x}_{t}-Z^{N,x}_{t}|\geq\delta\right\}\right)

\displaystyle\leq N\exp\left(-CN\delta\log(CN\delta)\right)).

The result clearly follows.
∎

6.2
The modified rate function
I^{\delta}

In this section, we define a modified rate function
I^{\delta}
and analyse how it relates to
I
. The main result is Corollary
6.6
below.

We define the following functional (Lemma
4.19
above). For
\delta>0
,
x\in A
,
y,\theta\in\mathds{R}^{d}
, let

\displaystyle\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(x,y,\theta)

\displaystyle:=\langle\theta,y\rangle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sup_{z=z^{j}\in A;|z-x|<\delta}\beta_{j}(z)\big(\exp(\langle\theta,h_{j}\rangle)-1\big),

\displaystyle L_{\delta}(x,y)

\displaystyle:=\sup_{\theta\in\mathds{R}^{d}}\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(x,y,\theta).

Obviously, we have

L_{\delta}(x,y)\leq L(x,y)

and for the respectively defined functional,
I^{\delta}
,

I^{\delta}\leq I.

We obtain

\displaystyle L_{\delta}(x,y)

\displaystyle=\sup_{\theta\in\mathds{R}^{d}}\Big\{\langle\theta,y\rangle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sup_{z^{j}\in A;|z^{j}-x|<\delta}\beta_{j}(z^{j})\big(\exp(\langle\theta,h_{j}\rangle)-1\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=\sup_{\theta\in\mathds{R}^{d}}\inf_{z^{1},\dots,z^{k}\in A;|z^{j}-x|<\delta}\Big\{\langle\theta,y\rangle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\beta_{j}(z^{j})\big(\exp(\langle\theta,h_{j}\rangle)-1\big)\Big\}

\displaystyle=\inf_{z^{1},\dots,z^{k}\in A;|z-x|<\delta}\sup_{\theta\in\mathds{R}^{d}}\Big\{\langle\theta,y\rangle-\sum_{j=1}^{k}\beta_{j}(z^{j})\big(\exp(\langle\theta,h_{j}\rangle)-1\big)\Big\}

(86)

\displaystyle=\inf_{z^{1},\dots,z^{k},|z^{j}-x|<\delta}\inf_{\mu\in\tilde{V}_{z_{j},y}}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\big(\beta_{j}(z^{j})-\mu_{j}+\mu_{j}\log\mu_{j}-\mu_{j}\log\beta_{j}(z^{j})\big)

(87)

\displaystyle=\ell(z^{*},\mu^{*}),

(88)

where we use the slight abuse of notation: for
z=(z^{1},\dots,z^{k})
,

\ell(z,\mu)=\sum_{j}\beta_{j}(z^{j})-\mu_{j}+\mu_{j}\log\Big(\frac{\mu_{j}}{\beta_{j}(z^{j})}\Big)

Note that
\ell(x,\mu)
depends on
x
only through the rates
\beta(x)
.

Here, Equation (
86
) follows from Sion’s min-max theorem, see e.g.
Komiya 1988
,
and Equation (
87
) follows by Theorem
4.9
. Equation (
88
) follows from Lemma
4.5
and the continuity of
\ell
and
\mu^{*}
(as a function in the state). We remark that
|z^{*}_{j}-x|=\delta
is possible.

In a similar fashion as before (cf. Proposition
4.21
), we define the sets

\displaystyle\Phi^{\delta}(K)

\displaystyle:=\{\phi\in D([0,T];A)|I^{\delta}(\phi)\leq K\},

\displaystyle\Phi^{\delta}_{x}(K)

\displaystyle:=\{\phi\in D([0,T];A)|I_{x}^{\delta}(\phi)\leq K\}.

In particular, we have
\Phi(K)\subset\Phi^{\delta}(K)
and
\Phi_{x}(K)\subset\Phi_{x}^{\delta}(K)
and
\Phi_{x}^{\delta}(K)
,
\Phi^{\delta}(K)
are increasing in
\delta
.

For technical reasons, we define for
m>0
,
z\in A
the rates

\beta_{j}^{m}(z):=\max\{\beta_{j}(z),1/m\}

and the corresponding functionals
L^{m}
and
I^{m}
by replacing the rates
\beta_{j}
by the rates
\beta_{j}^{m}
in the respective definitions.

We will need the following slightly stronger version of Lemma
5.6
, where again

\phi^{\eta}
is defined from
\phi
as in the lines before Lemma
5.6
.

Lemma 6.2
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3
holds. Let
K>0
and
\epsilon>0
. Then there exists an
\eta_{0}=\eta_{0}(T,K,\epsilon)>0
such that for all
\eta<\eta_{0}
there exists an
m_{0}>0
such that for all
m>m_{0}
and for all
\phi\in D([0,T];A)
with
I_{T}^{m}(\phi)\leq K
,

I_{T}(\phi^{\eta})<K+\epsilon,

where
\phi^{\eta}
is defined before Lemma
5.6
and satisfies
\|\phi^{\eta}-\phi\|\leq\epsilon
.

Proof.

We follows the first steps of the proof of Lemma Lemma5.1, where we replace
\mu^{\ast}(t)
by

\mu^{m,*}(t)
the optimal
\mu
corresponding to
(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))
and jump rates
\beta^{m}_{j}
. Now (
44
)
is replaced by

L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))\leq\ell(\phi^{\eta}(t),\mu^{m,*}(t-\eta_{r})).

(89)

We now choose
m>1/\eta
and deduce

|\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))-\beta_{j}^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))|\leq\frac{1}{m}+|\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))-\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))|\leq\delta^{\prime}_{K}(\eta),

(90)

where
\delta^{\prime}_{K}(\eta)=\eta+\delta_{K}(\eta)\rightarrow 0
as
\eta\rightarrow 0
by the (uniform) continuity of the
\beta_{j}
. We deduce from
(
89
) the following modified version of (
46
)

\displaystyle L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))\leq k\delta^{\prime}_{K}(\eta)+\sum_{j}\mu^{m,*}(t-\eta_{r})\log\frac{\beta^{m}_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))}{\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))},

(91)

since
\beta^{m}_{j}(\phi(t))>0
and
\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))>0
for
t\not=0
.

We recall that
\lambda_{1}
and the
v_{i}
’s have been defined in Assumption
2.3
(A3), and that the
\tilde{v}_{i}
’s,
\bar{v}_{i}
’s and
\hat{v}_{i}
’s
have been defined in the proof of Lemma
5.6
.

By Assumption
2.3
(B4), there exists a constant
\lambda_{4}>0
such that for
z\in B_{i_{r}}
(and
\eta<\eta_{2}\leq\eta_{1}
small enough,
depending upon
\lambda_{1}
and
\lambda_{2}
but not on
\phi
, except through
K
),

\beta_{j}(z)<\lambda_{4}\Rightarrow\beta_{j}(z+\eta\tilde{v}_{i_{r}})\geq\beta_{j}(z).

(92)

We now want to bound from above the second term in the right hand side of (
91
). If
\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))\geq\lambda_{4}
, then

\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))\geq 1/m
and therefore
\beta_{j}^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))=\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))
, so that the bound (
48
) holds.

Now consider the case
\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))<\lambda_{4}
.
We define the function
s(\delta):=\inf\{\beta_{j}(x)|\di(x,\partial A)\geq\delta\}
; hence (recall the continuity of the
\beta_{j}
and the compactness of
A
)

s(\delta)>0\text{ for }\delta>0
, and for
x\in A
,
\beta_{j}(x)\geq s(\di(x,\partial A))
.

We furthermore let

m_{0}=m_{0}(\eta,\lambda_{4})>\max\{1/\lambda_{4},1/s(\lambda_{1}\eta)\};

and recall that
\di(\phi^{\eta}(t),\partial A)\geq\lambda_{1}\eta
for
t\geq\eta
(cf. the discussion preceding Lemma
5.6
).

We let
m>m_{0}
.
Since
\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))<\lambda_{4}
, by (
92
),

\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))\geq\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r})).

By the definition of
s
, we have furthermore

\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t)))\geq s(\lambda_{1}\eta)\geq 1/m.

Combining these observations, we obtain

\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))\geq\max\{\beta_{j}(\phi(t-\eta_{r})),1/m\}=\beta_{j}^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))

and therefore

\log\frac{\beta_{j}^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}))}{\beta_{j}(\phi^{\eta}(t))}\leq 0.

(93)

From Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.5
, there exist (universal, i.e., independent of
x
and
m
) constants

B\geq B_{1}\vee B_{2}
,
B>1
,
C_{1}
,
C_{3}
such that

|y|>B\Rightarrow\forall x\in A,m,\,\,L(x,y),L^{m}(x,y)\geq C_{1}|y|\log|y|,

(94)

|y|>B\Rightarrow\forall x\in A,m,\,\,|\mu^{m,*}|=|\mu^{*}(x,y,m)|\leq C_{3}|y|.

(95)

Hence if
|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|\geq B
, we get, instead of (
52
),

\displaystyle L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq k\delta^{\prime}_{K}(\eta)+kC_{3}|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)}{\lambda_{4}}

\displaystyle\qquad\leq k\delta^{\prime}_{K}(\eta)+kC_{3}\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))}{C_{1}\lambda_{4}\log|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|}.

(96)

If
|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|<B
, Lemma
4.5
implies that

|\mu^{m,*}(t-\eta_{r})|\leq\tilde{C}B
for a universal constant
\tilde{C}>0
. Using Equations (
90
) and (
91
), we obtain

L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))\leq k\delta^{\prime}_{K}(\eta)+k\tilde{C}B\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)}{\lambda_{4}}.

(97)

Inequalities (
96
) and (
97
) imply

\displaystyle L(\phi^{\eta}(t),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))-L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))

\displaystyle\qquad\leq k(\eta+\delta_{K}(\eta))+k\tilde{C}B\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)}{\lambda_{4}}+kC_{3}\frac{2\delta_{K}(\eta)L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))}{C_{1}\lambda_{4}\log|\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r})|}

\displaystyle\qquad=:\delta_{1}(\eta)+\delta_{2}(\eta)L^{m}(\phi(t-\eta_{r}),\phi^{\prime}(t-\eta_{r}))

where
\delta_{i}(\eta)\rightarrow 0
as
\eta\rightarrow 0
,
i=1,2
. We now choose
\eta>0
such that

\delta_{2}(\eta)K<\frac{\epsilon}{4}\quad\text{and}\quad T\delta_{1}(\eta)<\frac{\epsilon}{4},

and choose
m>m_{0}(\eta)
; this yields

I_{T}(\phi^{\eta})<K+\epsilon.

∎

In the following, we show a relation between
L^{m}
and
L_{\delta}
.

Remark 6.3
.

It can easily be seen that Lemma
4.3
holds for
L_{\delta}
(with exactly the same proof). The same holds true for Lemma
4.12
and Lemma
4.17
.

Lemma 6.4
.

Let
\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) be bounded and
\epsilon>0
. Then there exists an
m_{0}>0
such that for all
m>m_{0}
, there exists an
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
\delta<\delta_{0}
and all
x\in A

y\in\mathds{R}^{d}
,

L^{m}(x,y)\leq\epsilon+(1+\epsilon)L_{\delta}(x,y)

Proof.

Let
m_{0}>0
,
m>m_{0}
and
\delta>0
. We let
\mu^{*}=\mu^{*}(z^{*},y)
be the optimal
\mu
associated to the optimal
z^{*}
according to
Equation (
88
). Then

L^{m}(x,y)-L_{\delta}(x,y)\leq\ell^{m}(x,\mu^{*})-\ell(z^{*},\mu^{*}).

(98)

Furthermore, we have by the uniform continuity of the
\beta_{j}
(cf. the proof of Lemma
6.2
),

|\beta^{m}_{j}(x)-\beta_{j}(z^{*})|\leq\frac{1}{m}+K(\delta)=:K_{1}(m,\delta).

(99)

Moreover, we note that if
\beta_{j}(x)<\frac{1}{m}-K(\delta)
, then

\log\frac{\beta_{j}(z^{*})}{\beta_{j}^{m}(x)}<0.

(100)

On the other hand, if
\beta_{j}(x)\geq\frac{1}{m}-K(\delta)
, then

\log\frac{\beta_{j}(z^{*})}{\beta_{j}^{m}(x)}\leq\log\frac{\beta^{m}_{j}(z^{*})}{\beta_{j}^{m}(x)}\leq\log\frac{\frac{1}{m}+K(\delta)}{\frac{1}{m}}\leq mK(\delta)=:K_{2}(m,\delta).

(101)

By Lemma
4.3
and Lemma
4.4
, there exist constants
B
,
C_{1}
,
C_{3}
and
C_{4}
such that for all
x\in A
and
y\in\mathcal{C}_{x}
,

\displaystyle L(x,y),L_{\delta}(x,y)>C_{1}|y|\log B\quad\text{if }|y|>B,

(102)

\displaystyle|\mu^{*}(y)|\leq C_{3}|y|\quad\text{if }|y|>B,

(103)

\displaystyle|\mu^{*}(y)|\leq C_{4}\quad\text{if }|y|\leq B

(104)

(note that the constants in Inequality (
102
) are independent of
\delta
).

For
|y|>B
, we have by Inequalities (
98
), (
99
), (
100
), (
101
), (
102
) and (
103
),

\displaystyle L^{m}(x,y)-L_{\delta}(x,y)

\displaystyle\leq kK_{1}(m,\delta)+kC_{3}|y|K_{2}(m,\delta)

\displaystyle\leq kK_{1}(m,\delta)+\frac{kC_{3}K_{2}(m,\delta)}{C_{1}\log B}L_{\delta}(x,y).

(105)

For
|y|\leq B
, we have by Inequalities (
98
), (
99
), (
100
), (
101
) and (
104
),

L^{m}(x,y)-L_{\delta}(x,y)\leq kK_{1}(m,\delta)+kC_{4}K_{2}(m,\delta)

(106)

The assertion now follows from Inequalities (
105
) and (
106
) by choosing
m_{0}
large enough,
m>m_{0}
and
\delta_{0}=\delta_{0}(m)
small enough such that

kK_{1}(m,\delta_{0}),kC_{4}K_{2}(m,\delta_{0}),\frac{kC_{3}K_{2}(m,\delta_{0})}{C_{1}\log B}<\frac{\epsilon}{2}.

∎

We directly deduce the following result

Corollary 6.5
.

Let
\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) be bounded and continuous. For all
\epsilon,K,T>0
, there exists an
m_{0}>0
such that for all
m>m_{0}
, there exists a
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
\delta<\delta_{0}
and all functions
\phi
with
I^{\delta}_{T}(\phi)\leq K-\epsilon
,

I^{m}_{T}(\phi)<K.

We now deduce from Lemma
6.2
and Corollary
6.5
the analog of Corollary 4.2 from
Shwartz and Weiss 2005
.

Corollary 6.6
.

Assume that Assumption
2.3
holds. Then for all
\epsilon,K>0
, there exists a
\delta_{0}>0
such that for all
\delta<\delta_{0}
,

\Phi^{\delta}_{x}(K-\epsilon)\subset\big\{\phi\in D([0,T];A)|\di(\phi,\Phi_{x}(K))\leq\epsilon\big\}.

Proof.

Let
\epsilon>0
and choose
m_{0}
,
m
,
\delta_{0}
,
\delta
according to Corollary
6.5
for
\epsilon/2
. Let
\phi\in\Phi_{x}^{\delta}(K-\epsilon)
. Then by Corollary
6.5
,
\phi\in\Phi_{x}^{m}(K-\epsilon/2)
. By Lemma
6.2
, there exists a
\tilde{\phi}
such that

\|\tilde{\phi}-\phi\|<\epsilon\quad\text{and}\quad I_{T,x}(\tilde{\phi})\leq K.

∎

6.3
Distance of
Y^{N}
to
\Phi^{\delta}

In this section, we derive a result about the distance of
Y^{N}
, defined by (
85
), to
\Phi^{\delta}
(Lemma
6.14
below).

We state the following elementary result (see, e.g.,
Roydon 1968
, Chapter 3, Proposition 22).

Lemma 6.7
.

Let
f:[a,b]\rightarrow\mathds{R}^{d}
be measurable with
f\not=\infty
almost everywhere. For all
\epsilon>0
, there exists a step function
g
such that
|g-f|<\epsilon
except on a set with measure less than
\epsilon
. Moreover the range of
g
is a subset of the convex hull of the range of
f
.

We define for
\delta>0
,
\phi:[0,T]\rightarrow A
and Borel-measurable
\theta:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathds{R}^{d}
,

I^{\delta}_{T}(\phi,\theta):=\int_{0}^{T}\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta(t))dt.

Lemma 6.8
.

Let
\log\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) be bounded.
For all absolutely continuous
\phi:[0,T]\rightarrow A
with
I^{\delta}_{T}(\phi)<\infty
and all
\epsilon>0
there exists a step function
\theta:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathds{R}^{d}
such that

I^{\delta}_{T}(\phi,\theta)\geq I^{\delta}_{T}(\phi)-\epsilon.

Proof.

As
I^{\delta}(\phi)<\infty
, there exists a large enough positive number
B
such that

\int_{0}^{T}\mathds{1}_{\{|\phi^{\prime}(t)|>B\}}L_{\delta}(\phi^{\prime}(t),\phi(t))dt\leq\frac{\epsilon}{3}

(107)

(cf. Lemma
4.17
and Remark
6.3
).
We set

\theta_{1}(t):=0\quad\text{if }|\phi^{\prime}(t)|>B.

By Lemma
4.12
(which holds true with
L
replaced by
L_{\delta}
, see Remark
6.3
–
this is where we need the assumption that the
\log\beta_{j}
are bounded),
there exists a constant
\tilde{B}
such that for all
x\in A
and
y\in\mathcal{C}
with
|y|\leq B
,

\sup_{|\theta|\leq\tilde{B}}\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(x,y,\theta)>L_{\delta}(x,y)-\frac{\epsilon}{6T}.

We set

D:=\{(x,y,\theta)|\ x\in A,y\in\mathcal{C},|y|\leq B,|\theta|\leq\tilde{B}\}.

The function
\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}
is uniformly continuous on
D
. Hence there exists an
\eta>0
such that for
|x-\tilde{x}|,|y-\tilde{y}|,|\theta-\tilde{\theta}|<\eta
,

|\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(x,y,\theta)-\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\tilde{x},\tilde{y},\tilde{\theta})|<\frac{\epsilon}{6T}.

By a compactness argument, we obtain a finite cover
\{\theta_{i,j},x_{i},y_{j}\}
of
D
such that

\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(x_{i},y_{j},\theta_{i,j})\geq L_{\delta}(x,y)-\frac{\epsilon}{3T}\quad\text{for }|x-x_{i}|,|y-y_{j}|<\eta.

(108)

We set

\theta_{1}(t):=\theta_{i,j}\quad\text{if }|\phi(t)-x_{i}|,|\phi^{\prime}(t)-y_{j}|<\eta

(with some kind of tie-breaking rule). Hence
\theta_{1}
only takes finitely many values. However, it is not clear whether
\theta_{1}
is piecewise constant.

We now choose
\tilde{\eta}
small enough such that
\text{Leb}[E]<\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{2}
implies that

\int_{[0,T]\cap E}L_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt\vee\int_{[0,T]\cap E}\sup_{|\theta|\leq\tilde{B}}\left(-\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta)\right)dt\ <\frac{\epsilon}{3}.

(109)

and

\min|\theta_{i,j}-\theta_{l,m}|>\tilde{\eta},\ \ \min|\theta_{i,j}|>\tilde{\eta}.

By Lemma
6.7
, there exists a step function
\theta_{2}
with
|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|<\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{2}
except on a set
\tilde{E}
with
Lebesgue measure
<\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{2}
.

Hence there exists a step function
\theta
which agrees with
\theta_{1}
except on
\tilde{E}
(modify
\theta_{2}
if necessary such that
|\theta_{1}-\theta_{2}|<\frac{\tilde{\eta}}{2}\Rightarrow\theta_{2}=\theta_{1}
on
\tilde{E}^{c}
). Note that
|\theta(t)|\leq\tilde{B}
, for all
t\in[0,T]
.

We conclude by collecting the approximations above:

\displaystyle I^{\delta}_{T}(\phi)

\displaystyle=\int_{[0,T]}L_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt

\displaystyle\leq\int_{[0,T]\cap\{|\phi^{\prime}(t)|>B\}}L_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt+\int_{[0,T]\cap\tilde{E}}L_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt

\displaystyle\qquad+\int_{[0,T]\cap(\{|\phi^{\prime}(t)|\leq B\}\cup\tilde{E}^{c})}L_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t))dt

\displaystyle{\leq}\frac{2\epsilon}{3}+\int_{[0,T]\cap(\{|\phi^{\prime}(t)\leq B\}\cup\tilde{E}^{c})}\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta(t))dt

\displaystyle=\frac{2\epsilon}{3}+\!\int_{[0,T]}\!\!\!\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta(t))dt-\!\int_{[0,T]\cap\tilde{E}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta(t))dt-\!\int_{[0,T]\cap\{|\phi^{\prime}(t)|>B\}\cap\tilde{E}^{c}}\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\!\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta(t))dt

\displaystyle\leq\epsilon+\!\int_{[0,T]}\!\!\!\tilde{\ell}_{\delta}(\phi(t),\phi^{\prime}(t),\theta(t))dt.

Indeed
\theta(t)=0
on the set
\{|\phi^{\prime}(t)|>B\}\cap\tilde{E}^{c}
, while (
109
) implies that the second integral in the next to last line is bounded by
\epsilon/3
.
∎

We next prove.

Lemma 6.9
.

Let
u:[0,T]\rightarrow\mathds{R}
be nonnegative and absolutely continuous and
\delta>0
. Then there exists an
\eta>0
, a Borel set
E\subset[0,T]
with

\text{Leb}(E)<\delta
and two finite collections
(J_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{+}}
and
(H_{j})_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}
of subintervals of
[0,T]
such that

[0,T]=E\cup\bigcup_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{+}}J_{i}\cup\bigcup_{j\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}H_{j}

and for all
i\in\mathcal{I}_{+}
,
j\in\mathcal{I}_{0}
,

\inf_{t\in J_{i}}u(t)>\eta,\quad\quad u(t)=0\text{ on }H_{j}\cap E^{c}.

Proof.

Given
t\in[0,T]
such that
u(t)>0
, let
\mathcal{O}_{t}
be the largest open interval containing
t
such that
u(s)>0
for all
s\in\mathcal{O}_{t}
.
Let
m_{t}=\max\{u(t),\ t\in\mathcal{O}_{t}\}
. Since
u
is absolutely continuous, there is a finite number of intervals
\mathcal{O}_{t}
such that
m_{t}>1/m
, for each
m\geq 1
.
Hence there are at most countably many open intervals
\{\mathcal{O}_{i},\ i\geq 1\}
where
u
is positive. Choose
M
large enough such that

\text{Leb}\left(\cup_{i=M+1}^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{i}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{2}.

For
1\leq i\leq M
, let
J_{i}\subset\mathcal{O}_{i}
be a closed interval such that

\text{Leb}\left(\mathcal{O}_{i}\setminus J_{i}\right)\leq\frac{\delta}{2M}.

Let

E=\left(\cup_{i=M+1}^{\infty}\mathcal{O}_{i}\right)\cup\left(\cup_{i=1}^{M}\mathcal{O}_{i}\setminus J_{i}\right).

Clearly Leb
(E)\leq\delta
. Let
\overline{M}
be the number of connected components of
[0,T]\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{M}J_{i}
.
For
1\leq j\leq\overline{M}
, let
H_{j}
denote the closure of the
j
–th connected component of

[0,T]\setminus\cup_{i=1}^{M}J_{i}
.
H_{j}
is an interval. Moreover

\displaystyle\inf_{1\leq i\leq M}

\displaystyle\inf_{t\in J_{i}}u(t)=\eta>0,\quad\text{and}

\displaystyle u(t)

\displaystyle=0,\quad\text{if }t\in H_{j}\cap E^{c}.

∎

We require this result for the proof of Lemma 4.6 of
Shwartz and Weiss 2005
. This is a (more general) variant of Lemma 5.43 of
Shwartz and Weiss 1995
(cf. also Lemma
6.8
).

Lemma 6.10
.

Assume that
\beta_{j}
(
j=1,\dots,k
) is bounded and Lipschitz continuous. Then for all
\phi
with
I_{T}(\phi)<\infty
and
\epsilon>0
, there exists a step function
\theta
such that

I_{T}^{\delta}(\phi,\theta)\geq I_{T}^{\delta}(\phi)-\epsilon.

Proof.

If none of the
\beta_{j}(\phi(t))
vanishes on the interval
[0,T]
, then the proof of Lemma
6.8
applies.
If that is not the case, we note that since
\phi
is absolutely continuous and
\beta_{j}
is Lipschitz continuous,
t\to\beta_{j}(\phi(t))
is absolutely continuous.
Hence we can apply Lemma
6.9
to the function
u(t):=\beta_{j}(\phi(t))
, and associate to each
1\leq j\leq k
intervals
(J^{j}_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{+}}

and
(H^{j}_{i})_{i\in\mathcal{I}_{0}}
. It is not hard to see that to each
\eta>0
one can associate
a real
\eta>0
, an integer
M
, a collection
(I_{i})_{1\leq i\leq M}
of subintervals of
[0,T]
, with the following properties

[0,T]=E\cup\bigcup_{1\leq i\leq M}I_{i},

with Leb
(E)\leq\delta
, and moreover to each
1\leq i\leq N
we can associate a subset
\mathcal{A}\subset\{1,2,\ldots,k\}
such that
